#6808. We Spent Two Years Testing Mattresses—These Are the Best for Back Pain

Every item on this page was hand-picked by a House Beautiful editor. We may earn commission on some of the items you choose to buy.

We found pressure-relieving picks for every sleep style and budget.

A great mattress turns your bedtime routine into the best part of your day. But a subpar mattress will make you dread turning in. We all deserve somewhere to rest that eases our aches and pains. After over two years of testing, the editors of House Beautiful are ready to reveal the best mattresses for back pain on the market.

We spent at least six months sleeping on each mattress below, evaluating their firmness, support, and relief on the pressure points. Every editor has a different sleep need: couples prioritize motion isolation, while stomach sleepers need enough firmness to keep their spine aligned.

Now, you may be thinking that mattresses designed to aid in joint support are going to be extremely expensive, and we won't lie to you; some of the mattresses on our list are pricey (the most costly one is just over $3,000). However, three of our top picks are well under $1,000, so if you want to save while still looking out for your back, you've come to the right place. Otherwise, feel free to splurge because these luxury mattresses are well worth it. And most of our picks come with a lengthy trial period, so you can take your time making sure it’s right for you.

Trust us; if you haven't been sleeping very well for more than a few weeks, your mattress may be to blame. Scroll through our list, read our honest thoughts on these mattresses, and add your favorite one to your cart.

Although Saatva's Rx mattress is "designed for sleepers with chronic back and joint conditions," the comfort level creates a dream sleep experience for all. Just ask tester Katelyn Lunders, who is a firm believer in the mattress after sleeping comfortably night after night for the past two years.

"As a combination sleeper with sciatica, I have found the Rx incredibly supportive and comfortable, whether I sleep on my back or side," she says. "Thanks to the mattress's seven (yes, seven) layers, the firmness isn't one note, like my former memory foam mattress. What sets the Rx apart from other mattresses on the market is its additional layers of pressure-relieving support. The Rx offers more support in the center third of the mattress to help align your spine while you sleep and has a gel-infused memory foam layer to support your lower back. Each section of the mattress works together to relieve pressure points and create a feeling of being cradled, which I love.

I currently sleep alone, so I get out to sprawl out across my Rx, but when I have shared the bed, I have experienced no issues with the edge support. Unlike some mattresses that will roll you off as you attempt to get out of bed in the morning, the Saatva Rx holds steady, thanks to firm spring coils around the mattress's perimeter. The coils provide support without sacrificing comfort due to the layer of foam on top of them.”

When you think of a mattress designed to soothe joint and back pain, you may picture a hefty price tag attached, but this Zinus find is the one exception. At only $230 (for a queen-size), this pick gets the job done for a fraction of the price. Just ask Lunders, who says, “I was personally so happy with my bed-in-a-box memory foam mattress that I told anyone who would listen to buy one, including my own family, who share my love of a good deal.” As for the back pain component, a firm mattress will do wonders, and Lunders notes that this mattress is definitely not soft. “I slept on my Zinus mattress for five years, and it never sagged. If anything, it became more comfortable with time,” she adds.

One more thing about the pressure relief component: Zinus enlisted its patented zoned pressure relief technology designed to help reduce even the smallest aches and pains that may arise throughout the night.

Not everyone has the budget for a luxury mattress that costs thousands of dollars, so if you want an under-$500 mattress that is supportive and holds up well over time, check out contributing writer Kelsey Mulvey's pick.

“As far as I’m concerned, Allswell’s Luxe Hybrid Mattress offers the best bang for your buck,” Mulvey says. “I mean, the King mattress I have costs under $500! It’s sometimes hard to find bedding for that price point! Layers of foam—think a copper-infused memory foam and a shock-absorbing high-density support foam—are paired with an eight-inch coil unit to create the perfect medium-firm base. It’s soft and cloud-like yet provides the right amount of structure, which my husband and I really appreciate.”

“We first got this mattress back in 2019, and it has endured a pandemic, a move, an engagement, and many lazy nights watching movies in bed. While we have experimented with different mattress pads and bed frames, this pick has stayed in tip-top shape and has no obvious sags or dips.”

Tester Samantha Jones was waking up with shoulder and back pain most mornings. But when she tested out the cooling Leesa Sapira Hybrid mattress, that changed.

“Its squishy memory foam layers on top provide a sink-in feeling like you're climbing into a bed at a luxury hotel,” Jones says. “The individually wrapped coils below did their job of providing support for my hips, shoulders, and back, making the bed comfy yet not like quicksand. Notoriously someone who tosses and turns for hours before falling asleep, I've actually found that I drift off to sleep faster on the Sapira Hybrid than my previous, firmer mattress model.”

“I have been waking up with significantly less back pain on the Sapira Hybrid. As an avid runner, I'm accustomed to waking up with tight muscles after a workout. But because the individually wrapped coils contour to my body and provide adequate support for my hips at night, I've noticed less lower back pain and stiffness. The same is true for my shoulder pain: The balance of squish and support has helped eliminate shoulder discomfort.”

Senior Commerce Editor Marina Liao’s customizable Naturepedic mattress allowed her to build a bespoke sleep routine. “I initially slept on the cushion-firm layers and liked them, but after a few weeks, I noticed they didn’t feel as comfy, especially when my sciatica flared up,” Liao says. “The layers suddenly felt too soft for me. Naturepedic offers a 100-night sleep trial, which gives you ample time to test the mattress thoroughly. I switched my layers for the ‘extra firm’ option and immediately liked their feel much better.”

“The mattress feels soft when you’re lying on it, and though you might intuitively think this means you’ll wake up with an achy back, that hasn’t been my experience. The layers ensure you feel supported from the top of your head to your neck, spine, and even hips. I’ll also add that this mattress has a 12-inch height, so it is taller than my old mattress, which makes me feel elevated off the ground and like I’m sleeping on a cloud.”

As a stomach sleeper, Social Media Editor Bianca Bonetti is used to waking up with back pain. She sleeps comfortably since switching to the pillow-top Estate, and to her, it feels like every pressure point is supported.

“Considering I sleep in a position that is bad for my back, it’s kind of a miracle that I haven’t woken up with soreness since sleeping on the Estate mattress,” Bonetti says. "Unless it’s the day after a tough workout. I was hoping to get the best of both worlds by still feeling cocooned and like I’m sleeping on a cloud, but looking out for the health of my back and posture,” Bonetti says. “I love that I get the low back support I need without sinking into the mattress. I do think I would have purchased a medium pillow top if it had been an option for the full size, but I was pleasantly surprised by my love for a firm mattress.”

Shopping Editor Jessica Dukes is pretty picky about her bed and bedding. Comfort and support have to come first, as she has sciatica that doesn’t respond well to poofy mattresses. So the Casper Dream was her ideal switch.

“Night after night, I have found this mattress to be extremely comfortable,” Dukes says. “I also didn't notice too much of a difference between how it felt the first night and how it feels several weeks in, so if you don't want to wait up to 48 hours to start using it, I think that's okay. Despite my back issues, the medium firmness of the memory foam is a delight, which is high praise considering how picky I am. The mattress gives me a slight ‘sinking in’ feeling, but it's supportive at the same time. The result is balanced comfort, and whether I fall asleep on my side or back, I wake up with zero back pain. As for my husband's tossing and turning, I barely feel him move, as those coils do a great job of minimizing the effects.”

If you've ever seen a video of someone dropping an uncooked egg onto a purple grid and being amazed that the egg didn't immediately explode, you're familiar with the mattress brand Purple. That famous purple grid lives beneath the surface of most of its products, as it's the backbone (pun intended) of its creations. The grid in question is what gives you that cradled support ideal for anyone who likes a little extra TLC on their backs. “Given that I weigh much more than an egg, I was skeptical. But after a year of sleeping on the mattress, I'm officially a convert,” says Associate Shopping Editor Janae McKenzie.

“Just above the supportive coils is the purple GelFlex grid, the feature Purple is best known for. The Premier has the thickest grid, so it gives the softest hold,” McKenzie adds. “It's the first mattress I've tested that lives up to its promise to cradle me. Plus, the isolated coils provide pinpoint relief, almost like the mattress can sense where I need extra support and deliver it.” With Purple, you don't really have to settle for a firm mattress (if that's not your thing) to protect your back.

There may not be white-glove delivery involved for the Nectar mattress, which comes in a box and unrolls in 24 hours for you to sleep on, but it feels just as nice as some of the other luxury models on the market. "I grew up sleeping on rock-hard mattresses, so I felt dubious about foam options, thinking they would feel too soft and hurt my back. My Nectar has completely changed my mind about this. Now I can't even go back to sleeping on the surfaces I've previously slept on," Liao says. Nectar offers its mattress in three different tiers with various features: Nectar, Nectar Premier, and Nectar Premier Cooper, which you can read all about on the website. For a brief overview, though, the models differ in height and offer relief for different types of sleepers.

"We opted for just the Nectar in a queen size, which is a 12-inch mattress with cooling capabilities," Liao says. "I would love to take credit for finding the mattress, but it was actually my husband who bought it six years ago while we were still dating for his apartment. I asked him why he bought it, and he said, 'It had good reviews on Reddit or something. I don't remember, but I love it.' I have to concur because it does check the boxes that are important to us, which are comfort and support, as both of us have sciatica.

The mattress is indeed medium-firm, and even after countless nights of rest, it has maintained this feel. When we get up to use the bathroom at night, neither of us feels the other waking up, which speaks to the multiple layers of the Nectar that minimize motion transfer. Both of us have experienced periodic night sweats on this mattress (when our AC breaks or when we're stressed about work), so it's not as cooling as the description says, but with the right sheets (linen!), it does feel cooler. However, if you run extra hot, you may want to upgrade to the Nectar Premier Copper, which has triple-action cooling power.I really love my Nectar, and when it comes time to change it, I'll just buy a Nectar again. In fact, several people who have slept on this bed actually ended up purchasing it right after, including my husband's cousin, who was then pregnant with her third child. She immediately bought it for her and her husband after napping on it!"

How We Selected the Most Comfortable Mattresses

As you might've guessed, the phrase "most comfortable" is subjective when you're talking about a mattress. We all have different sleep preferences, so there isn't a one-size-fits-all approach to shopping for one. In order to know which mattresses are actually comfy and which are not, we started by asking our in-house experts: House Beautiful's very own editors. Many of us test beds for a living (it's part of our jobs), so we only recommend the ones we truly stand by. Of course, in our research, some editors candidly said they don't like their mattresses, so you won't find those named on our best-of list.

After we had our pool of editors who love their mattresses, we made sure everyone had slept on theirs for at least six months. You can only tell if the mattress is truly comfortable over a period of time by sleeping on it every day, which is why plenty of the brands on our list have long sleep trial periods. Additionally, you can rest assured that everyone on the House Beautiful team has different sleeping habits, which translates nicely to a well-rounded curation. You'll find comfortable mattresses for those with back pain, comfortable mattresses for side sleepers, and even comfortable mattresses for the whole family (pets included!). Every editor also provided pros and cons for their mattresses, so you have all the details to make the most informed purchase decision.

What to Look for in the Most Comfortable Mattress

There are several qualities to take note of when you're shopping for a comfortable mattress. They are:

Mattress Type: You will notice that the mattresses on our list are either foam or a hybrid. Foam mattresses are made of memory foam and other layers of foam without any springs or internal structures. Meanwhile, hybrid mattresses typically have coils or an innerspring along with a comfortable layer on top. This layer can be made from various materials, from polyfoam or latex to memory foam. One type isn't better than the other since personal preference will play a role here.

Memory foam is known for conforming to your body's shape and cradling you while providing support. You don't have to worry about squeaky springs (there are none), and the dense foam layers make it hypoallergenic because dust and mites will find it hard to penetrate the deep layers. Some drawbacks of memory foam, though, include trapping heat while you sleep and less edge support. This means you sink in if you sit on the edge of the mattress since memory foam doesn't have coils for support.

On the flip side, since a hybrid has a coil support system, it will feel sturdy and supportive. There's less chance of your mattress sagging over time, and hybrids can support heavier weights. The coils, too, allow for more airflow, so your mattress holds in less heat. Some downsides to hybrids: they are often more expensive than foam mattresses, and they are usually heavier to maneuver.

Firmness: Everyone has a different tolerance for firmness; some prefer a softer feel, while others like a rock-hard mattress. Firmness is calculated by how far one sinks into a mattress. And remember: firmness does not equate to support. The latter is determined by how the mattress helps a sleeper maintain spinal alignment. So, you can have a firm bed but not have it be supportive for your back. To test for support in person, lie on the mattress for at least 10 to 15 minutes—the mattress should push back slightly to support your body. You will know if the mattress supports your body or not after sleeping on it for a few nights. If you get aches and pains anywhere, that means the mattress is not supportive.

Temperature Regulation: If you're a hot sleeper, you know how important it is to find a mattress that is more cooling, which often comes in the form of a hybrid model. (The coils don't trap as much heat as multiple layers of an all-foam mattress.) Look for a mattress with elements like a breathable cover made of cotton or natural latex that regulates body temperature. Mattresses with gel-infused memory foam are also good indicators that they'll cool you as you sleep.

Motion Isolation: If you sleep alone, this probably isn't important to you, but if you sleep with a partner or pet, pay attention to this category. A mattress that can reduce motion transfer will help you get a good night's rest, whether your partner is getting out of bed early for a workout (and you want to sleep in) or your pet is jumping on/off the bed throughout the night. All the picks on our list have excellent motion isolation, but a simple test you can do at home is to put a cup of water on the bed. Press your hands down on one side or even dramatically plop onto the bed—if the glass of water doesn't spill or the liquid barely moves in the cup, the mattress has good motion isolation.

Trial Period: Almost all mattress brands will offer a lengthy trial period for testing out your new piece. You can't sleep on a mattress for a week and expect it to still feel the same months down the line! We love brands that offer a year-long trial, but chances are, when you buy a mattress and it works, you likely won't go through the hassle of returning it (unless it is really terrible!). Make sure to check the return policy and how to return your mattress if needed, as this can affect whether or not you want to buy it.

What to Consider When Buying the Most Comfortable Mattress

Don't forget these personal preferences when shopping for a mattress. They will play a role in what you end up buying.

Budget: When buying any big piece of furniture, price is always a concern. You may not have the budget to spend thousands of dollars on a comfy mattress, but that doesn't mean you should sacrifice your sleep for a more inferior model. Thankfully, our editors all had different budgeting constraints in mind when buying mattresses, so our list contains comfy mattresses that give you the most for your buck to more luxurious options that make you feel like you're staying at a five-star hotel. You can find a quality and supportive mattress for under $500!

Sleeping Position: Now that you're familiar with the different types of mattresses, what is your sleep position? If you're a side sleeper, for instance, memory foam and hybrids are good for pressure-relieving comfort. If you're a heavier sleeper, you may want to opt for a hybrid since the coils will provide more support. If you like your mattress to feel very stiff, then an all-foam option may not be the best for you. Stomach sleepers, like one of our editors, will need medium-firm to firm support, which foam and hybrid will provide. We highlighted the comfiest mattresses for the different types of sleepers above, but again, you won't know until you try one out for yourself. Make sure to read through our notes and the product description, as a good brand will note whether or not this is good for all types of sleeping positions or just caters to one.

Body Type: Similar to how your height affects the type of mattress you buy (you don't want your feet dangling off!), your weight will also factor in the purchase decision. Sleepers who weigh 130 pounds or less can handle a softer mattress because they don't sink too deep into the mattress. If you weigh between 130 and 230 pounds, go for a medium-soft to medium-firm mattress. For those who weigh more than 230 pounds, a firmer mattress with extra support is ideal.

How to Pick the Most Comfortable Mattresses for Pain

Chances are, if you're looking for a comfortable mattress, it's because you've been sleeping on a mattress that gives you back pain, or you have a pre-existing condition that makes picking a mattress trickier. Here, we break down what to look for if you have:

Back pain: Look for a mattress with a medium-firm to firm surface. Studies show that this might effectively reduce back pain in individuals. Mattresses like the ones from Saatva even spotlight that they're good for people with back pain and are chiropractor-approved. Look for these types of callouts in your mattress as you shop.

Hip pain: The best mattresses for hip pain offer firm lumbar support with gentle pressure relief. The mattresses should have a supportive edge and be high enough to allow your hips to remain above your knees when you're sitting on them. Memory foam is often good for hip pain as it contours to your body, but keep in mind that if you're a stomach or back sleeper with hip pain, this material might allow too much sinkage, and your spine won't be properly aligned—going with a hybrid model then is more beneficial.

Shoulder pain: For people with shoulder pain, we didn't forget about you! Look for memory foam or latex mattresses that will provide pressure relief and support as they contour to your body the best. In particular, if you're a side sleeper with shoulder pain, opt for a softer mattress vs. a firmer one. Also, consider switching out your pillow, as the wrong one will misalign your spine, causing both neck and shoulder pain. Shop our best pillows for neck pains.

House Beautiful editors are experts in all things bedding, from having written about the softest sheets to reviewing the most luxurious mattresses. Our editors have tested and thoroughly reviewed popular mattresses from the likes of Nolah, Saatva, Purple, and more, plus traded notes with one another so we can recommend the best options to readers. For this story on comfortable mattresses, since the topic is entirely dependent on personal preferences, we asked our fellow editors for their top-rated picks that they themselves own and have slept on for months to years at a time. There is no better indicator that a mattress is comfortable if you've had it for a long time, and it helps alleviate any pain! We spent over a week making sure the list was packed with a variety of brands and mattress types so readers could find the one that most closely matches their own personal preferences. Every mattress on this list is thoroughly vetted by the team.

#6806. These 5 Brands Are Making the Holiday Pajama Sets I Want Most—I've Rounded Up the Best Ones

I'd happily make these my everyday uniform if I could!!

We may earn commission from links on this page, but we only recommend products we love. Promise.

Sure, decorating my house, lighting a fresh pine-scented candle, and listening to Christmas music all day, every day get me in the holiday spirit, but what really does it for me? Available in the brand’s beloved TENCEL™ Modal and flannel sets, there’s a range of festive yet chic designs to choose from, including polka dots, bows, holly berries, and more.

#6802. Skyler Gisondo on His New Fashion Campaign—and Dressing Like Harrison Ford in the ’90s

Every product was carefully curated by an Esquire editor. We may earn a commission from these links. Here’s how we test products and why you should trust us.

The Primetime star is gearing up in Bonobos and getting ready for a very big 2027.

Skyler Gisondo is remarkably easy to talk to. If you’ve seen him on one of your various screens—whether as Decoy Dan from Primetime, Jimmy Olsen from Superman, or maybe just that meme of him from a decade-old red-carpet photo—you probably assumed as much. It’s the big smile, the friendly eyes, the freckles, the general sense of wholesomeness. But I can confirm it, because even though I know we’ve got a relatively brief window of time to discuss his new campaign with men’s fashion brand Bonobos over Zoom, we spend the first few minutes of our chat just … shooting the shit. It feels like catching up with a buddy you haven’t seen in a while. Relaxed. Natural.

Thankfully, the vibe continues as we start digging into matters of style. Gisondo tells me he’s always been interested in dressing well but hasn’t always known where to start. “I would see a cool dude walking down the street or something, and think, ‘Man, I want to look like that guy.’ But I never knew how. I always felt lost,” he says. He describes his 20s as a decade of “desperately trying on shoes that didn’t fit or something.”

He turned 30 recently, though, and “I really feel like over the last couple of years, I’ve homed in on my sense of style, what I feel good and confident in.” A very good time, then, to star in a fashion campaign—especially considering this seems like a breakout moment for the actor. He’s been working consistently since he was a kid, but his performance in Primetime as a young actor who gradually realizes just how toxic the entertainment industry can be has clearly launched him to another level. And he still has his upcoming role in Focker-in-Law this November and a couple more appearances as Jimmy Olsen (in Man of Tomorrow on the big screen and The People v. Gorilla Grodd on television) on the docket for 2027.

Over the course of our conversation, we touch on everything from dressing like a cowboy (a mistake) to using Harrison Ford in the ’90s as your style North Star (a very good idea). We also chat about the stuff he’s stolen from sets for his own wardrobe, the psychological validation of joining the Shrek franchise, and why, after Primetime, we can expect more creative big swings in the future. Read on for a few highlights.

This interview has been condensed and edited.

“It’s ultimately about your own confidence. I feel like the sweet spot for fashion is when what you look like reflects how you feel inside—when you get to this equilibrium and it allows you to be a more fully realized version of yourself. We all grow and our sense of fashion evolves with us but ultimately like I feel like there should be some current that runs through all of it. The stuff I’m drawn to is the more timeless, classic stuff. I look at Harrison Ford in the ’90s and I’m just like, it doesn’t get cooler, you know?”

“For a while, for whatever reason, me and every actor I know were like trying to look like a cowboy. And it just hit me one day: ‘None of us have ever worked a day in our lives on a cattle ranch, man.’ I don’t even know how to use a hammer! Like, what are we doing? Can we just be ourselves?”

“I’m pretty active. I surf and hike a lot when I’m at home. And I love walking; if I have an hour or two between meetings, I’ll just walk a few miles and take calls. I want something comfortable and functional, and I find a lot of vintage workwear just hits that and fits me well. And the story is so compelling to me. The idea of ‘Who’s worn this? What were they getting up to?’ ”

“What drew me to the campaign and to the clothes is that there’s a kind of timelessness to that style. My mom thought I looked amazing in the photos. My girlfriend too. My grandma and my sisters thought I looked great. It’s this very accessible and sort of classically cool thing they have going on. And I felt like me. I felt confident—so much so that I stole several items from the set.”

“With shows and movies, the fear is that it’s a one-of-one item that you might need back for another season. But that’s when you hit them with the ‘pretty, pretty please?’ You say, ‘I swear to God, I’ll guard it with my life.’ And they say, ‘Okay, but you can’t tell anybody.’ With a campaign, it’s probably easier because they’re excited for you to have it and represent. But probably 80 percent of my closet is basically just clothes stolen from characters and sets. It’s fun because you get to try on a bunch of versions of yourself and learn what you like.

“For instance, with Jimmy Olsen in the first Superman movie, they did a fantastic job with this retro-future thing. And then in The People v. Gorilla Grodd, they went a little more vintage-y in a way that felt more like me, Skyler, personally. I tried to steal almost the entire closet.”

“Well, right now I’m doing press for Primetime and Focker-in-Law. I’m doing another movie for A24 called Goblin that I’m not sure how much I can say about; I really don’t want to get fired. But I’m so excited. It’s the most insane script I think I have ever read, and it’s definitely a big swing. We have Man of Tomorrow coming out in the summer, and then Shrek 5. The number of texts I got when I got Shrek … ‘You made it, man! Oh my God! I had no idea you made it!’ I was like, ‘Thank you. Yeah, baby! Shrek 5!’

“But ultimately, it’s a very exciting moment because it’s the first time I have people asking, ‘What do you want to do?’ And I’m like, ‘Damn! I never really thought about that!’ I feel like Primetime lit a fire under me in a real way. And even though I’ve kind of been doing this my whole life, I feel a sense of drive that I’ve never felt really in this way before. So, yeah, man, I’m excited.”

#6801. ‘Dancing With the Stars’ Celeb and Pro Plan to Reveal Secret Relationship After the Season Ends

Rumor has it that Dancing With the Stars’ front-runner Ezra Frech and pro Hailey Bills are secretly dating, and while neither of them have confirmed...literally anything (in fact, Ezra claimed he was “single” just this week!), a source says they plan to come clean about their relationship once the show ends.

“Hailey and Ezra are getting even flirtier and more serious as each week goes on,” an insider tells The Sun. “As the season continues, they are getting more comfortable sharing their feelings about each other with the cast. They still want to keep it on the DL publicly so Ezra can focus on the last few weeks of finishing this season strong. So they won’t be posting together anytime soon. But if they’re still together after the season ends, they’ll proudly show off each other then.”

They added that “Hailey is so smitten by Ezra and he treats her with so much respect and is in awe of her. They both share a similar sense of humor and just laugh and laugh and laugh together backstage.”

Meanwhile, Ezra recently told Emma Slater and Sharna Burgess during a TikTok Live on Disney Night, “I’m single. I’m locked in on dancing.”

FYI, a source previously told The Sun that “They are being really careful not to post each other right now because they don’t want the chatter about them potentially dating to overtake everything else. Ezra is really serious about making it to the finals and even winning this show. At the end of the day, he has the mindset of an Olympian. So he’s not letting a crush get in the way of winning the show. So they’re being strict about not posting together until the season is over.”

#6799. The 15 Best Moisturizers Perfect for Mature Skin

Every product on this page was chosen by a Harper's BAZAAR editor. We may earn commission on some of the items you choose to buy.

Nourishing favorites from brands like Augustinus Bader and ZIIP come expert-recommended

There are plenty of ways to treat signs of aging, but among the most effective is also the simplest: Striking the right moisture balance for your skin. Proper hydration can help reduce the appearance of fine lines and dullness, while also improving skin laxity over time. While almost any face cream can boost hydration, the best moisturizers for mature skin provide targeted, preventative treatments in a jar.

Key ingredients, such as peptides, retinoids, and others, work together to target specific concerns. “Retinoids or plant-based alternative bakuchiol improve and prevent fine lines, increase cellular turnover, improve skin texture and elasticity, and [reduce] discoloration,” dermatologist Morayo Adisa tells Bazaar. “Look for antioxidants such as vitamin C and E to help with collagen production and decrease oxidative stress. Hyaluronic acid, glycerin, and urea to increase skin hydration, softening the appearance of lines and wrinkles. Also, look for an emollient such as squalane or shea butter.”

Several moisturizers feature at least one of these transformative ingredients, so how do you know which one is right for you? To help narrow down your options, we asked Adisa, along with other dermatologists, for their thoughts on some of the best moisturizers for mature skin.

Neutrogena continuously proves that you don't have to spend a ton of money to reap the benefits of wrinkle-smoothing ingredients. This night cream targets the primary causes of wrinkles, uneven tone, and loss of elasticity with a firming and plumping lipid complex called Hexinol, brightening vitamin C, and moisturizing ingredients such as shea butter and glycerin.

Key ingredients: vitamin C, glycerin, hexinol

The clinical studies on this gel cream after four weeks of use are pretty impressive. Co-signed by dermatologist Hadley King, this moisturizer works at a cellular level to restimulate collagen production, diminish dark spots, and significantly smooth deep-set wrinkles.

“It contains humectants and emollients like squalane, as well as niacinamide, which improves tone and texture, and willowherb, which has senolytic properties to help promote skin longevity,” says King. The brand's proprietary combination of plant stem cells targets exosomes, energizes skin through NAD+, and decreases inflammation.

Key ingredients: squalane, niacinamide, willowherb, plant stem cells

Bring the spa home: This do-it-all hydrator makes it a breeze to give mature skin a refresher. Valmont’s Prime Renewing Pack leaves skin with a healthy glow in minutes and uses a creamy formula that reduces fine lines and wrinkles while seamlessly smoothing texture. Use a thin layer of the Pack as a quick five-minute mask, or a thicker layer for a more intensive treatment.

Key ingredients: peptides, RNA liposome, triple DNA complex

Sisley Paris's Black Rose cream is a go-to choice for beauty lovers and makeup artists alike, thanks to its excellent priming capabilities. The star ingredients, alkekengi calyx extract and padina pavonica, work in tandem to plump skin that's lost volume. There's also hibiscus flower extract and alpine rose extract, which help erase dullness, making your skin look more vibrant and glowy before you apply any makeup.

Key ingredients: black rose oil extract, black rose aqueous extract, alpine rose extract, alkekengi calyx extract, padina pavonica extract, hibiscus flower extract, shea and camelina oils

If you’re looking for a longer-lasting alternative for skin hydration, allow us to introduce you to Neuraé’s Harmonie The Sleeping Mask. The nourishing mask is created to rejuvenate dull, dry skin while reducing redness over time. Plus, its emollient-rich formula is the perfect addition to your beauty routine in colder months.

Key ingredients: gardenia jasminoides extract, shea butter, kokum butter, babassu oil

Here’s a top-rated moisturizer from Sulwhasoo that minimizes dullness, fine lines, wrinkles, and dryness simultaneously. The hypoallergenic cream has a whipped texture that works for a range of skin types and delivers up to 24 hours of hydration.

Key ingredients: Korean herb extract, Jisun firming complex, white mulberry, jujube, Chinese skullcap

Augustinus Bader is another worthy splurge, and in a few uses, you'll understand why. The Rich Cream utilizes a proprietary blend of TFC8, which features squalane, argan, sunflower seed, and evening primrose oils to stimulate cellular renewal and deliver targeted ingredients to skin cells.

Key ingredients: evening primrose oil, argan oil, sunflower seed oil, squalane, glycerin

If you’re already a fan of ZIIP’s devices, this moisturizer from the brand deserves a place in your rotation. The brand’s high-tech essential works with your skin’s electrical activity, along with ingredients like encapsulated collagen peptides, argireline, and more, to smooth expression lines while boosting firmness and radiance after six weeks of use.

Key ingredients: collagen peptide moisturizer with argireline amplified peptide solution, marine samphire bioactive

The 2:4:2 in the names refers to the trifecta of two percent ceramides, four percent cholesterol, and two percent fatty acids that work together to rebalance the skin and even out elasticity irregularities that cause sagging, roughness, and enlarged pores. It's a deeply moisturizing formula that replenishes and seals in moisture to help skin look and feel smooth and plump.

Key ingredients: ceramides, cholesterol, fatty acids

You'll see impressive skin-firming results with many of the moisturizers on this list, but this one is specifically designed for those who don't want to wait weeks to reap the benefits of their cream. Over time, the formula stimulates the sirtuin protein within the skin to boost collagen, hyaluronic acid, and elastin production.

Key ingredients: instant tightening sugars, micro-hyaluronic acid, glycerin, sunflower seed oil, grapeseed oil, shea butter

Dermatologist Marisa Garshick notes that some moisturizers contain ingredients that help support collagen production, thereby improving the appearance of fine lines and wrinkles while firming the skin. “A great option is the Mother Science Molecular Genesis barrier repair moisturizer, which uses their patented ingredient malassezin to strengthen the skin barrier, while also containing other ingredients like hyaluronic acid, growth factors, copper peptides, lipids and squalane, which together help to boost hydration, reduce the appearance of fine lines and wrinkles, and offer antioxidant benefits against free radical damage,” she explains.

Key ingredients: malassezin, copper peptides, squalane, hyaluronic acid, lipid concentrate

Mature skin can still experience breakouts, but this formula, which King also recommends, replenishes lost moisture without clogging pores or making the skin oilier. The formula looks to dimethicone for moisture and adaptogenic Chinese herbs to strengthen compromised skin barriers.

Key ingredients: dimethicone, ginseng, gotu kola, dongquai

Another one of Adisa's top choices is this rejuvenating cream from Andalou Naturals. The formula utilizes bakuchiol, a gentler yet equally effective alternative to retinol. “Bakuchiol works to reduce the appearance of fine lines and wrinkles and overall leaves the skin feeling rejuvenated,” Adisa explains. It also features a fruit stem cell complex and super antioxidants to restore and protect the skin barrier. "This has a nice, lightweight whipped consistency that quickly absorbs," notes Adisa.

Key ingredients: bakuchiol, resveratrol, goji berry, aloe vera

This rich moisturizer, created by dermatologist Ben Johnson, is rich in barrier-protecting oils and antioxidants. The cream also contains an effective blend of phosphatidylcholine, raspberry seed oil, and shea butter to keep skin nourished. However, if you require even more moisture, consider layering the brand's face oil, Osmosis Nourish, on top after application.

Key ingredients: phosphatidylcholine, raspberry seed oil, shea butter, apricot oil, blueberry

Here's a top-rated cream that works overnight to smooth and tighten as you snooze. The formula not only evens skin's texture but also repairs its barrier while visibly lifting sagging skin. Plus, since you won't need more than two pumps of the favorite to cover your entire face, a little goes a long way.

Key ingredients: retinol, bakuchioil, vitamin C, hyaluronic acid, antioxidants

Adisa noted earlier that retinoids or the plant-based alternative, bakuchiol, are ideal for improving and preventing fine lines, increasing cellular turnover, smoothing skin texture, and enhancing elasticity—so check ingredient lists for these. Garshick says to also look for moisturizers like hyaluronic acid and glycerin, along with ingredients to support the skin barrier, such as ceramides and squalane, and peptides or growth factors to help with firmness. “Antioxidants like vitamin C can help to protect against environmental stressors, while niacinamide may be added to help calm and soothe the skin,” Garshick notes.

“As we age, the skin barrier weakens, contributing to increased moisture loss, and there is decreased production of sebum, hyaluronic acid, and ceramides, key ingredients that help to keep the skin moisturized. This makes the skin more prone to dryness and dullness,” Garshick explains.

Johnson adds that skin dries out with age primarily due to a loss of circulation. “As the skin ages, it loses about one percent of circulation each adult year, so at 50, the skin has 25 percent less circulation,” says Johnson. “This leads to fewer nutrients, lipids, and immune cells, which result in a porous, dehydrated barrier.”

Incorporating a moisturizer for mature skin helps hydrate the skin while supporting the skin barrier. “Some moisturizers may also contain ingredients that help to support collagen production, improving the appearance of fine lines and wrinkles while also firming the skin,” notes Garshick.

Johnson advises steering clear of alpha-hydroxy acids because they can dry out and damage the skin. Ceramides, while generally good for sealing in moisture, can also be problematic. “They have a feedback-loop effect on the skin that results in them slowing down your epidermal turnover rate,” the expert says. You should also ease off on harsh exfoliants, which can damage the skin barrier and speed up moisture loss, leaving skin dry and crepey.

#6798. What Does Non-Acnegenic Mean, Exactly? Dermatologists Break Down the Skincare Term

And I thought I knew all the acne buzzwords...

We may earn commission from links on this page, but we only recommend products we love. Promise.

When I was a young girl in the early 2000s, there was always a bottle of that iconic blue Cetaphil Gentle Skin Cleanser in my parents’ house. It was probably the only thing I washed my face with for the first 10 years of my life. But what stuck with me the longest (and perhaps was an early sign that I was destined to be a beauty editor) was that, right under the product name, the label said “non-comedogenic.” This new word perplexed me. I vividly remember trying to figure out how to pronounce it as I washed my face every evening, finally asking my mom during my very short nighttime skincare routine. She told me how to say it and what it meant in a way that a 7 year old would understand, and that was that. I was all up to speed.

Fast forward two decades later, and I’m sitting here, perplexed once again, as I learn about a new-to-me, related-to-non-comedogenic term: non-acnegenic. I’ve been seeing the word pop up here and there when it comes to an acne-friendly skincare routine, but I had to know, what actually makes it different from non-comedogenic? Is it a marketing buzzword, or is whether or not a product is non-acnegenic really important to pay attention to? I had far more questions than 7-year-old me, so I turned to two dermatologists to explain everything.

Let’s start with some good news: The definition of non-acnegenic is honestly pretty straightforward. “Non-acnegenic means a product has been formulated and tested so it won’t trigger acne breakouts, including the red and inflamed bumps and pustules most people picture when they think of acne,” says board-certified dermatologist Mona Foad, MD.

If you have acne-prone skin and/or are currently trying to figure out how to get rid of acne, Dr. Foad calls it “one of the most useful labels you can look for.” This is because brands “typically test these products on people with acne-prone skin over several weeks with a dermatologist evaluating the skin for any new breakouts,” she explains.

However, using a non-acnegenic skincare product isn’t a 100 percent guarantee that it won’t cause a breakout, unfortunately. “Think ‘less likely to invite a breakout,’” says board-certified dermatologist Mona Gohara, MD. This label means the product is unlikely to cause or worsen acne, but there’s no way for a brand to promise that result for everyone.

Non-acnegenic and non-comedogenic are different—but there’s plenty of overlap, Dr. Gohara says. Non-comedogenic, as you may already know, means that a product “is designed not to clog pores,” says Dr. Foad, who notes that a comedone is the technical term for a clogged pore. “Non-comedogenic addresses clogged pores, while non-acnegenic addresses the full picture of acne, including inflammation.”

Here’s what I found really interesting: “A product can avoid clogging pores and still irritate the skin enough to trigger inflammatory breakouts,” says Dr. Foad. Just because a product is non-comedogenic doesn’t mean you won’t get a breakout after using it. But that doesn’t mean using a non-comedogenic product can’t be helpful in avoiding acne, either. “Clogged pores are the starting point for acne, so preventing them is the first line of defense,” Dr. Foad explains. “When a product carries this label, the brand is claiming that its formula won’t block your pores and set the stage for breakouts.” Think of it this way: Acne-prone skin wants protection from potential pore cloggers and acne triggers alike. So, if you’re shopping for acne-prone skin, Dr. Foad recommends looking for products that are both non-comedogenic and non-acnegenic.

Oh, and another important thing to note: “Neither means the product actually treats acne,” says Dr. Gohara. Remember, these labels just mean that you’re putting skincare or makeup on your face that is less likely to set off a breakout.

As discussed above, non-comedogenic and non-acnegenic do have accepted definitions in the beauty industry. But there’s one super important caveat: Just like any non-medical claim on an over-the-counter beauty product, neither of these terms is regulated by the FDA. “This is one of the biggest sources of confusion I see,” says Dr. Foad. Because the FDA doesn’t define or regulate either of these, “there’s no standard test brands are required to use,” she says.

What does that mean, exactly? “One company might test a product on dozens of acne-prone volunteers over several weeks, while another might rely on ingredient lists alone,” explains Dr. Foad. “That’s why these labels are a helpful starting point, not a guarantee.” Your best bet is to look for brands that are transparent about their clinical and consumer testing, so you can review those and decide for yourself if a product looks promising enough to buy.

There’s no one magical ingredient that automatically makes a product non-acnegenic, but these are often used in products meant to support acne-prone skin:

Again, the inclusion of these ingredients doesn’t guarantee you won’t break out after using them. If you have an allergy or sensitivity to anything on this list, you should avoid it.

On the flip side, here are some ingredients to avoid, as they can trigger breakouts and clogged pores:

In general, for those who are acne-prone, “I’d steer them away from heavy, oil-based foundations, concealers, and facial balms,” says Dr. Gohara. Instead, look for lightweight foundations and other makeup products that don’t contain the ingredients above.

Of course, anyone who’s acne-prone, oily, or combination may want to ensure their beauty products are non-acnegenic. But beyond that, “they’re also essential for patients using prescription acne treatments like tretinoin or benzoyl peroxide, which can be drying and require a moisturizer that won’t undo their progress,” says Dr. Foad.

While pimples are often associated with teenagers, you may also benefit from non-acnegenic products as you age: “I also see many women dealing with hormonal acne in their 30s and 40s, and through perimenopause and menopause, when breakouts can appear for the first time in years,” she adds.

Catharine Malzahn is a contributing beauty writer at Cosmopolitan with five years of experience researching and writing skincare and makeup stories.

Catharine Malzahn is a contributing beauty writer at Cosmopolitan and was previously the beauty assistant at Good Housekeeping, Woman's Day, and Prevention.

The Reflecting Pool has become a little too thematic of the president’s second term.

And, in closing, the president talked to the nation about ... algae.

Why the president decided to deliver his closing address before a symbol of everything that was wrong with his ever being president—cronyism, incompetence, and howling mendacity—is not for small minds to ponder. Maybe he counts the war on algae to be one of the nine or ten wars he’s won. But he also defeated the mass vandalism that (I think) James Comey loosed upon his massive piece of pool.

He looked every one of his 80 years and maybe a couple of Chuck Grassley’s 93 years besides. His delivery was erratic and enervated. This took a lot of the weight out of his usual stem-winding confabulations.

(Here the president is quoting the latest “exclusive” from the New York Post. And since the president and his administration are still stonewalling on the Epstein files, one must assume their House of God probably has a VIP room.)
